§4.3 Pooling-benefit and energy diagnostics
Figure 4: (a) Absolute pooling gap J(¯p) −Φ(λ) separates the two regimes cleanly with no overlap. (b) The scale-free
ratio PB(x) = (J(¯p) −Φ)/Φ overlaps considerably between regimes and is not a useful separator. (c) Internal energy
E(λ) = P
i λiH(p∗
λ, pi). Both the absolute gap and E(λ) separate the regimes; PB does not.
All experiments are fully specified by the model checkpoint, prompt sets (including the perturbation procedure for
correlated neighborhoods), temperature, the (α, n)-grid, and Monte Carlo seeds. We use deterministic seeds for next-
token sampling and for coincidence Monte Carlo trials, and report standard errors across neighborhood replicates.
Model-scale ablation predictions and protocol.
To test whether the mixed coincidence structure reflects model qual-
ity rather than merely vocabulary statistics, we repeat the core diagnostics on EleutherAI/pythia-160m [15] alongside
the gpt2 baseline [74], both running on a single CPU. The pipeline is checkpoint-agnostic, so larger models (Pythia-1.4B,
Llama-3.2-1B) drop in by changing one identifier; we report Pythia-160M because it is the largest tested model that fits
the local CPU testbed used to verify reproducibility.
We predictthat: (i) Z(α) is larger onthe morecapablemodel forcorrelatedprompts(higherprior overlapfromgreater
model confidence); (ii) the coincidence horizon Ψ(α) log n extends (coincidences persist to finer vocabulary subdivisions);
(iii) the top-K approximation converges faster (the probability head dominates more). These observations would jointly
support the claim that partition-function diagnostics track model quality: a better model produces more coherent geo-
metric mixtures, sharper coincidence horizons, and more concentrated effective vocabularies, so the mixed coincidence
structure should sharpen with model scale.
G.2
KL-barycenter identity: numerical exactness, α-sweep, and energy diagnostic
Per-candidate J(r) check.
Across the six neighborhoods of Figure 8, the per-candidate J(r) = P
i λiD(r∥pi) con-
firms that the geometric-mixture row sits exactly at Φ(λ) on every neighborhood, while the arithmetic mixture, single
priors, and uniform candidates sit strictly above.
Pooling-gap and energy diagnostics.
Figure 4 reports the per-neighborhood absolute pooling gap J(¯p) −Φ(λ), the
scale-free pooling-benefit ratio PB, and the internal energy E(λ) for an extended benchmark across 30 + 30 neighbor-
hoods (the realized version of the body’s six-row Paris benchmark).

CAVEAT. The PB ratio does not vanish in the correlated limit. As both priors agree, J(¯p) and Φ shrink at comparable rates,
so PB = (J(¯p)−Φ)/Φ stays bounded away from zero. A naive prediction is that PB →0 for correlated neighborhoods;
the empirical PB histograms in Figure 4(b) overlap considerably and are not a useful regime separator. The absolute gap
is the right quantity for the prediction; PB is a useful normalized diagnostic only when one wants to discount the overall
scale of Φ.

α-sweep ablation.
We additionally sweep λ along an interior path of the simplex (one prior, two priors, asymmetric
(0.7, 0.2, 0.1), symmetric center, asymmetric reverse (0.2, 0.2, 0.6)): both Φ(λ) and PB(λ) respond smoothly to the λ di-
rection and the regime separation is preserved at every interior point. This is direct empirical evidence that the multipliers
α behave as natural local coordinates for scanning typicality across observation schemes.
G.3
Coincidence-probability protocol and Poisson diagnostics
Random subdivision / cascade model.
We evaluate coincidence probabilities in the (multi-way) random subdivision
/ cascade model used in the theory. Fix integers m, n and prior distributions pi ∈∆(X). For each population i ∈
{1, . . . , W}, we generate n i.i.d. items; in each of m rounds, each item receives an i.i.d. label in V drawn from pi. Given
multiplicities α = (α1, . . . , αW ) ∈NW , an α-coincidence occurs if some cell (an m-tuple label) contains αi items from
every population i.
The expected number of α-coincidences is λ(n, m; α) =
QW
i=1
  n
αi

(Z(α))m. We compare two standard proxies
for the coincidence probability p⋆(n, m) := Pr(at least one α-coincidence):
pMarkov(n, m) := min{1, λ(n, m; α)},
pPois(n, m) := 1 −exp{−λ(n, m; α)}.
TheMarkovproxyisarigorousupperbound. ThePoissonproxyisapproximatebutoftenaccurateinsparselarge-alphabet
regimes where coincidence indicators are rare and weakly dependent. (The Poisson proxy is always lower, because in
general 1 −e−λ ≤λ.)
Monte Carlo protocol.
For each grid cell (n, m), each Monte Carlo trial samples an n × m matrix of labels per popu-
lation from pi, hashes each row into a cell key in Vm, and tests for α-coincidences by intersection across populations. We
use T = 30 trials per grid cell on 4 correlated and 4 diverse neighborhoods (so T · 8 = 240 trials per grid cell aggregated
across regimes) and report the resulting empirical probability ˆpemp(n, m) together with the per-trial coincidence-count
distribution. The grid is sized so the threshold curve m ≈Ψ(α) log n passes through it for both regimes.
Beyond the binary coincidence indicator, we record the count of α-coincidences per trial and compare the empirical
distribution to a Poisson(λ) distribution. In sparse regimes the count distribution should be close to Poisson; deviations
arise primarily in high-overlap neighborhoods where dependence between collision events is stronger.
Phase-transition heatmap construction.
The body figure (Figure 1) reports three benchmarks at W = 3, α =
(2, 2, 2) on a continuous-log n-grid; the warm/cool boundary in each panel follows the theoretical threshold m ≈
Ψ(α) log n across the entire grid.

§4.6 Effective support and tail engagement (GPT-2, q=2)

λ). Correlated neighborhoods drop
below the diagonal (concentration); diverse neighborhoods sit above (anti-concentration). (b) Distribution of the integer-

0.95 across neighborhoods.
Effective support: integer- vs. simplex-multiplicity diagnostics.
For a Rényi order q > 0 and any positive multi-
plier vector α, the multi-prior Rényi pooling gap admits two natural specializations: with integer multiplicities α = 1
(sum W) it counts the Rényi entropy of the geometric product relative to the sum of single-view entropies; with simplex
weights λ = 1/W it counts the Rényi entropy of the geometric mean relative to the average single-view entropy. We
report both, because they encode different aspects of pooling.
Information collapse.
Figure 6(a) plots the simplex-version effective support: average single-view log S2(pi) on the
x-axis vs. pooled log S2(p⋆
λ) on the y-axis, with the diagonal as the no-collapse reference. The empirical picture on gpt2
priors is: both regimes sit slightly above the diagonal – the geometric mean of next-token distributions over paraphrased
prompts is consistently a little broader than the average single-view distribution, because even paraphrased gpt2 priors
disagree enough at the tail to spread the geometric mean. Diverse neighborhoods sit further above (mean simplex-∆λ,2 =
−0.44) than correlated ones (mean −0.23); the simplex-∆is therefore a useful regime-separator, but its sign does not
match the naive “correlated ⇒collapse” intuition for paraphrased LLM next-token priors at this concentration level. The
integer-multiplicity variant ∆1,2 is positive in both regimes (mean 9.88 for correlated and 7.44 for diverse) because the
W · log Sq(pi) term dominates – it remains the cleaner “how concentrated is the multiplicative product” diagnostic and
inherits the correct sign convention. The histogram in Figure 6(b) confirms that the integer-∆histograms still overlap
meaningfully but with the regime separation consistent with the prediction.
Tail engagement at fixed q.
We treat K (how many vocabulary symbols are kept in the top-K approximation) as
a resolution parameter: small K looks only at the head, large K engages the tail. For each neighborhood we regress
log(1−fλ(K)) on log K over the intermediate range and correlate both the slope and the stabilization point K0.95 with
log Sq(p∗
λ). Figure 6(c) shows the relationship at q = 2: across the 60 neighborhoods, Spearman ρ(K0.95, log S2(p∗
λ)) =
0.26 (p = 0.048). The signal is modest – both regimes sit in a narrow log S2 band on gpt2 priors – but in the predicted di-
rection: neighborhoods whose pooled typical distribution has larger effective support require larger K to reach the same
certified head fraction. This turns the top-K bracket into a quantitative head-vs.-tail diagnostic without any reference to
a representation-space geometry.
Model-scale ablation.
We repeat the core diagnostics on EleutherAI/pythia-160m (Figure 7). The pooling-benefit
ratio and the pooled effective support are tightly correlated across models on the same neighborhoods, demonstrating
that the diagnostics are functions of the prior structure rather than artefacts of a particular checkpoint. On the same
prompt set, mean Φ for correlated/diverse moves from (0.61, 2.00) on gpt2 to (0.66, 1.85) on pythia-160m; the regime
separation is preserved with mild model-dependent rescaling. Larger checkpoints (Pythia-1.4B, Llama-3.2-1B) drop in

G.5
Autoregressive overlap trace and POS-stratified analysis
Fix W = 3 instruction prefixes π(i) (bare, “Please answer the following:”, “Provide a concise factual completion:”), prefix
them to a common base prompt x to form x(i) = π(i)∥x, sample a continuation y1:T from the bare-prompt model under
temperature T = 1 for T = 80 tokens, and at every step t recompute the W next-token priors pi(· | ht) from each
prefix-augmented context ht := x(i)∥y1:t−1. We track two interpretable scalar fields:
• Stepwise full-consensus probability (α = 1): Zt(1) = P
v∈V
QW
i=1 pi(v | ht), the probability that W inde-
pendent next-token draws all match.
• Coincidence (Rényi/Chernoff-style) divergence (λ = ( 1
W , . . . , 1
W )): Φt = −log P
v
Q
i p1/W
i
(v | ht).
For each trace we record log Zt(1) and Φt together with top-1 agreement events and a Rényi pooling band Φt −∆α,2
that reports the q = 2 effective support correction. We replicate this pipeline across five disjoint base prompts – physics,
weather, music, history, and analysis – and aggregate POS statistics across all five traces to give the closed/open-class
comparison statistical power.
POS-tagged analysis.
We annotate each position t with the spaCy part-of-speech (POS) tag of the generated token yt
[48] and partition positions into closed-class tokens C (DET, ADP, PUNCT, CCONJ, SCONJ, AUX, PART, PRON) and open-class
tokens O (NOUN, VERB, ADJ, ADV, PROPN, INTJ, NUM). The prediction [83] is that closed-class positions carry higher cross-
prior consensus – equivalently, lower Φt – because syntactic constraints dominate, while open-class positions exhibit
lower consensus because semantic content introduces disagreement among priors.
EMPIRICAL FINDING. Pooled across 5 disjoint base prompts (nC = 185, nO = 195 tokens), the means are ΦC = 7.4×10−3
vs. ΦO = 7.3 × 10−3 (Welch p = 0.93); log Z(1)C = −3.77 vs. log Z(1)O = −4.25, a small shift in the predicted
direction (closed-class positions are higher in the multiplicative consensus), modest at the single-continuation sampling
used for this trace. A better-powered sweep settles the question. Across gpt2 (124M) and Pythia at 70M, 160M, and
410M parameters, crossed with W ∈{3, 8} instruction prefixes and pooled over five base prompts with three sampled
continuations each (§H.8), the full multiplicative consensus log Zt(1) acts as a single-feature open- vs. closed-class POS
sensor in every configuration: closed-class positions carry the higher consensus, with closed-vs-open ROC-AUC between
0.70 and 0.81 and Welch p < 10−8 throughout, while the coincidence divergence Φt stays weaker (AUC 0.61–0.71). The
57


## Page 58


sensor is present already at the smallest model and at W = 3 and does not sharpen monotonically with W or model scale:
it is the full-consensus feature, not larger scale, that the diagnostic relies on.
Autoregressive sequence coincidences.
As a complementary check, the entire m-token continuation can be treated
as a single label: Yi ∼Pi(· | x(i)) on Vm, where Pi is the model’s autoregressive distribution. Sampling n continuations
per prompt, hashing the resulting m-tuples, and estimating the α-coincidence event on Vm provides the sequence-space
analogue. The clean formula λ = (Q
i

)Z(α)m need not hold exactly due to across-position dependence, but the mea-
sured coincidence rates remain a complementary check of how “coincidence-like” model continuations are in sequence
space.
G.6
Real hg38/ENCODE benchmark: per-locus narrative
ThefourcCREelementclassesusedasregulatoryannotationsarePLS(promoter-likesignature), pELS(proximalenhancer-
like signature), dELS (distal enhancer-like signature), and CA-CTCF (CTCF-bound chromatin-accessible regions); these
are the four bedfile categories of the SCREEN Registry V4 release of the ENCODE Project.
Locus selection rationale.
The four pharmacogene loci on hg38/GRCh38.p14 (CYP2D6, CYP3A4, ABCB1, CYP2B6,
each with ± 200 kb flanks) are directly relevant to drug discovery and pharmacology: CYP2D6 metabolizes many com-
monly prescribed drugs, CYP3A4 is involved in metabolism of approximately half the drugs in use today, CYP2B6 me-
tabolizes xenobiotics including cyclophosphamide and ifosfamide, and ABCB1 is a broad-specificity drug efflux pump
implicated in multidrug resistance. The epigenomic side used the human hg38 ENCODE/SCREEN Registry V4 cCRE
annotation, specifically the PLS, pELS, dELS, and CA-CTCF BED files; on this panel these labels cover 18.9% of bases in
total (PLS 1.2%, pELS 4.2%, dELS 12.6%, CA-CTCF 0.9%). For each window we formed (i) a sequence prior over 6-mers,
with |Xseq| = 4096, and (ii) a joint prior over (6-mer, cCRE-class) tokens, with |Vjoint| = 5 × 46 = 20480 after adding
a background class. This is a diagnostic benchmark rather than a held-out prediction task: because the joint alphabet
includes the cCRE label itself, the goal is to test whether the coincidence calculus resolves known matched genomic and
epigenomic structure on real hg38 sequence.
KL-barycenter identity and local overlap.
The arithmetic-mixture penalty J(¯p) −Φ was consistently larger on
the joint alphabet than on sequence alone, rising from 0.103 nats in stable local neighborhoods to 0.122 at regulatory
boundaries and 0.272 in diverse triplets. Sequence-only gaps were smaller (0.054, 0.056, 0.123). On real human loci, then,
the multiplicative barycenter again extracts a sharper common-overlap signal once regulatory labels are folded into the
alphabet.
Windowed divergence traces on real regulatory boundaries (per-locus).
Using midpoint cCRE-class transitions
as the referenceboundary set, the pooled sequence-onlytrace achievedAUC0.567, whereasthe joint sequence-cCRE trace
reached 0.897. The gain held on every locus: 0.536 →0.875 on CYP2D6, 0.571 →0.922 on CYP3A4, 0.599 →0.901
on ABCB1, and 0.492 →0.861 on CYP2B6. In mean-value terms, Φjoint increased from 0.199 in stable neighborhoods to
0.241 at boundaries, while Φseq moved only from 0.169 to 0.173. This improvement is expectedly aided by using the same
cCRE annotation in the joint tokens, but that is precisely the point of the present diagnostic: the overlap trace remains
well-behaved and highly sensitive on matched genomic and epigenomic structure, not only on LLM prompts.
The trace is not a single signal track in disguise.
A reasonable objection is that Φt might just be a thresholded version
of one of the underlying annotation channels (Registry coverage, HepG2 open fraction, GC content). It is not. Across the
four locus-level landscapes, the median absolute Spearman correlation between the k = 12 breathing trace and any single
occupancy or composition signal is only 0.110; the maximum is 0.352. The trace is reacting to how the regulatory and
compositional structures are arranged across the three-window neighborhood, not to occupancy at any one window.
High-k explicit-prior attribution.
At higher k the exact priors atomize – by k = 16 the per-window support is essen-
tially unique, and exact k = 28 priors collapse to near-uniform background / Registry / HepG2 splits. Smoothed explicit
priors over the same atoms (background, Registry, HepG2 accessibility, promoter grammar, motif program) preserve bio-
logical structure and become an attribution object: the fitted weights ˆλt = arg minλ∈∆m−1 DKL(qt ∥pλ
t ) at the selected
centers locate which biological view drives each bend. At the CYP2D6 promoter / open notch the fitted k = 28 weights
58


## Page 59


are HepG2 0.79, promoter 0.13, Registry 0.06, motif 0.01, background 0.00 – the bend is overwhelmingly accessibility-
driven with secondary promoter-grammar support. The four loci tell complementary stories with the same machinery:
CYP2D6 a sharp promoter / open notch, CYP3A4 a broad dELS basin where the k = 28 fit becomes Registry / motif-
led, ABCB1 a HepG2-only shoulder (HepG2 weight 0.66, motif 0.22), and CYP2B6 a shared pELS / open plateau where
Registry, HepG2, promoter, and motif simultaneously carry appreciable mass.
Coincidence probabilities, top-K mass, and support.
The coincidence tools of the main LLM experiments also
transferred cleanly. On the joint alphabet, the three-way coincidence divergence C(1,1,1) rose from 14.01 nats in stable
neighborhoods to 14.59 at boundaries and 17.45 in diverse triplets, while the Poisson proxy for at least one three-way
coincidence in one round with n = 100 tokens per window dropped from 0.559 to 0.370 and then 0.026. At the same
time the support geometry broadened substantially: K95/|Vjoint| increased from 0.755 to 0.765 and then 0.887, and the
pooled order-2 effective support S2 increased from 1.27 × 103 to 1.55 × 103 and then 3.06 × 103. Real hg38 windows
behavemuchlikethelarge-vocabularylanguageneighborhoods: localconsensusexists, butheterogeneousneighborhoods
force the barycenter deeper into the tail.
Synthesis: DNA breathes in regulatory units, not clause units.
The four-locus pharmacogene scan establishes that
the coincidence trace moves up and down on real human loci in a genomically structured way; the weak simple correla-
tions show that the trace is not a thresholded annotation channel; the high-k explicit-prior fit shows why k must be used
differently in segmentation (k = 12, the breathing geometry) vs. attribution (k = 28, which biological view explains the
bend). The synthesis is the genomic counterpart of the language picture in Figure 3(a)–(b): the trace is well-behaved at
low k (where it acts like a structure statistic) and biologically interpretable at high k (where the smoothed explicit priors
localize the active biology).
A complementary view, with a different prior alphabet, sharpens this picture. Replace the (6-mer × cCRE-class) joint
of Table 2 with the cell-type accessibility categorical itself: an eight-cell ENCODE DNase panel (GM12878, K562, HepG2,
HeLa-S3, IMR-90, H1, MCF-7, A549; continuous read-depth-normalized bigWig signal at GRCh38). On three loci cho-
sen for sharp constitutive-promoter / cell-type-specific-enhancer juxtaposition (MYC, ALB, GAPDH, ∼1.45 Mb total at 2
kb windows / 256 bp stride), the same W = 3 partition-function machinery yields a boundary-detection trace whose
pooling-gap diagnostic |J(¯p) −Φ| reaches AUC 0.938 on ALB (bias-corrected accelerated bootstrap (BCa) 95% confi-
dence interval (CI) [0.901, 0.959]), 0.894 on MYC, and 0.863 on GAPDH. The mean across loci (0.899) meets and the peak
(0.938, on the liver-specific ALB locus) strictly exceeds the 0.897 joint-cCRE benchmark of Table 2, on a different alphabet
over different windows — evidence that the partition-function diagnostic generalizes to cell-type-resolved priors and is
not a feature of the (6-mer, cCRE) construction alone. A clean structural fact about the calculus on this prior: the di-
vergence DKL
 ¯p ∥¯ppanel−uniform

to a panel-uniform reference is anti-correlated with the boundary truth, because at a
W = 3 neighborhood spanning one constitutive (near-uniform) and one cell-type-specific (peaked) window the pooled
barycenter is a mixture closer to uniform than either pure end. The pooling gap and the divergence to a constant reference
therefore do different jobs, mirroring the segmentation-vs-attribution split that Φt at low k vs. the high-k explicit-prior
fit produces above. The per-locus cell-type-prior traces, the full prior-construction methodology, and the bootstrap-BCa
caveats are detailed in §H.19.
Data source note. Coordinates were taken from the GRCh38.p14 NCBI Gene records for CYP2D6, CYP3A4, ABCB1,
and CYP2B6. Regulatory labels came from the hg38 SCREEN Registry V4 PLS, pELS, dELS, and CA-CTCF downloads.
The accessibility layer is the previously cached HepG2 peak set; motif-hit positions come from a Polygraph-style local
motif scan.
H
Empirical evaluation supplement
This supplement contains detailed verifications of the empirical claims made in the main text. A reader who wants the
numeric substantiation of any one of the four central observational claims — the triangle identity at machine precision,
the absolute pooling gap as a single-feature regime separator, the phase-transition heatmap aligned with m ≈Ψ(α) log n,
and the per-position autoregressive trace structure — should consult the corresponding subsection below, alongside its
figures and per-row table.
The subsections group into five families:
• Large-alphabet gpt2 diagnostics (the body’s Section 5 at full detail): the KL-barycenter pooling benchmark
(§H.1); the coincidence phase-transition shape (§H.2, with the synthetic Zipf panel of the body figure at §H.6); the
59


## Page 60


certified Hölder bracket (§H.3); effective support and the disclosed W ≥10 sign flip (§H.4); the POS-stratified au-
toregressive trace (§H.5); the wider 30+30 neighborhood regime-ordering benchmark (§H.13); and the continuum-
α Riemann-sum convergence on gpt2 priors recovering that ordering at the continuum limit (§H.14).
• Real small-alphabet information-theory corollaries: guesswork log-moment versus Rényi exponent (§H.15);
the finite-n M-ary Rényi–Chernoff bound with Arimoto–Sibson coupling on three multi-class datasets (§H.16); the
multi-priorPAC-Bayescoincidencebonus(§H.20); andtheheld-outnext-tokenNLLofgeometricversusarithmetic
pooling (§H.22).
• Genomic regulatory diagnostics: the multi-prior coincidence of three independent sequence-to-function pre-
dictors localizing the active regulatory element at base-pair resolution (§H.17), with its cell-type-ranking corollary
(§H.18); the cell-type-prior boundary-detection scan on three loci (§H.19); and the hg38/ENCODE pharmacogene
boundary-detection re-benchmark (§H.21).
• Operational supplements: the Erdős–Rényi run-length threshold (§H.9); the de-Poissonization small-n correc-
tion (§H.10); the atoms-theorem NNLS recovery (§H.11); the bulk-genomics log Z stability at W = 40 priors
(§H.12); and the three-regime autoregressive trace (§H.7).
• Machine-precision verification of the identity and all its algebraic specializations, collected as descriptive prose
in Appendix I, with the per-cell residual battery released with the companion code.
H.1
KL-barycenter identity on gpt2 prompt neighborhoods
We verify the central numerical claim of §5.2 on gpt2 (|V| = 50257): the triangle identity J(r⋆
λ) = Φ(λ) from Theo-
rem 2.1 holds at float64 precision across correlated and diverse prompt neighborhoods, and the absolute pooling gap
J(¯p) −Φ separates the regimes while the scale-free pooling-benefit ratio PB = (J(¯p) −Φ)/Φ does not.
Benchmark.
The benchmark is a six-row ”Paris” benchmark at W ∈{3, 10, 100}:
• Correlated rows: Paris_Corr_W for W ∈{3, 10, 100} — paraphrases of ”The capital of France is”.
• Diverse rows: Paris_Div_W for W ∈{3, 10, 100} — country-history templates over a 109-country list (”The
history of {country} is characterized by”).
Per-row metrics.
For each row, we extract gpt2 log-probability vectors at the final prompt position over the full
|V| = 50257 vocabulary and compute, in the log domain, Φ(λ) = −log Z(λ) at λ = 1/W, the geometric mixture r⋆
λ,
the arithmetic mixture ¯p = P
i λipi, the uniform reference, and the per-candidate J(q) = P
i λi D(q ∥pi). Figure 8
summarizes the per-row pooling quantities. The per-row triangle-identity residuals |J(r⋆) −Φ| are all at machine pre-
cision (one ULP of float64; detailed below), and the per-row energies E(λ) = Φ + H(r⋆
λ) are 6.165949, 7.589216,
8.680269 on the correlated rows and 5.339331, 5.350843, 5.444302 on the diverse rows (at W = 3, 10, 100), tracking
Φ.
Triangle identity.
The maximum residual across the six rows is 1.332 × 10−15 (within one ULP of float64, 2.22 ×
10−15), and the mean residual is 5.49 × 10−16. This reproduces the value stated in the main text, “residual bounded by
2.22 × 10−15 with mean 6.16 × 10−16” (§5.2), at the six-row benchmark. Every row satisfies the machine-precision
threshold, the diverse rows have residual at the very low end of the float64 range, and the highest-W correlated row
(Paris_Corr_100) has the largest residual — consistent with the log-sum-exp accuracy degrading mildly as more log-
priors are summed, but still within one ULP.
Absolute gap orders the regimes; PB ratio does not cleanly.
Across the candidate distributions the geometric mix-

0.05).

Scope.
On this six-neighborhood benchmark (3 correlated and 3 diverse at W ∈{3, 10, 100}), the most central quan-

H.2
Coincidence phase-transition heatmap on a gpt2 prior
We verify part (ii)–(iv) of Theorem 3.1 on a gpt2-prior benchmark: the empirical multi-way coincidence probability over
the (n/|V|, m) plane should transition across the theoretical threshold curve m ≈Ψ(α) log n. The realized benchmark
is a diverse W = 12 country-history neighborhood at coincidence multiplicity α = (3, . . . , 3) with stop-word masking;
the body’s three-benchmark version is Figure 1.
Setup and stop-word masking.
The W = 12 next-token priors are extracted from a diverse country-history prompt
set. To prevent the heatmap from saturating in the high-frequency token regime (where very common tokens like “the” /
“ and” would trivially produce coincidences across all priors at every m), the top-100 highest-mean-probability tokens —
averaged across the priors — are masked from the empirical hit-counting. The number of priors W and the multiplicity
α are chosen so the transition sits mid-grid rather than pinned at the bottom: at W = 12, α = 3 the coincidence is hard
enough that the threshold mcrit lands near the center of the displayed m-range.
Thesample-sizeaxisisageometricgridfocusedonthetransitionstrip(n ∈[1.9k, 10.7k]), andm ∈{1, 2, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25}.
For each n we draw one multinomial count vector per prior, mask the top-100 stop-word indices, and record a single-cell
hit if all W priors have count ≥α = 3 at some shared non-stop-word token. The per-n hit probability ˆp1(n) is averaged
over T = 400 trials; the per-m probability is 1 −(1 −ˆp1(n))m, which crosses 1
2 at mcrit(n) = ln 2/[−ln(1 −ˆp1(n))].
Reading the figure.
The empirical transition tracks the predicted curve m ≈Ψ(α) log n of Theorem 3.1: at small
n the threshold sits high (priors rarely co-occur at multiplicity 3), and it descends as n grows and coincidences become
common. A single diverse neighborhood is realized here, so the per-regime Ψ-comparison (correlated vs. diverse) lives
in the body’s three-benchmark heatmap (Figure 1); this single-neighborhood realization establishes the transition shape
and its agreement with the predicted threshold.
61


## Page 62


Figure 9: Coincidence phase-transition heatmap (gpt2, W = 12 diverse country-history priors, multiplicity α =
(3, . . . , 3), top-100-token stop-word mask). Color encodes Pr[ m-coincidence ]; the dashed red curve is the empirical
threshold m ≈Ψ(α) log n with fitted Ψ ≈1.48. With W = 12, α = 3 the transition sweeps through the center of
the displayed grid — no m-coincidence below and to the left, saturated above and to the right — so the threshold reads
at mid-grid rather than pinned at m ≤2 as it is for the easier W = 10, α = 2 setting. The body figure (Figure 1) uses
W = 3 at full scale.

We verify the certified two-sided Hölder bracket on Z(α) from §5.4: the per-K bracket on Z obtained from a head-only
subset SK ⊂V should tighten rapidly as K grows. The realized benchmark is two prompt-set rows (Paris_Corr_3 and
Paris_Div_3, both at W = 3, λ = (1/3, 1/3, 1/3)) at six head sizes K ∈{10, 50, 100, 500, 1000, 2000}, with the
order q = 2 chosen for the divergence units of the bracket.

We instantiate the effective-support / tail-engagement diagnostics from §5.4 on the same 6-row gpt2 benchmark used

Table 4 summarizes the per-row results.
Thecollapse-vs-expansionsignbehavioratscale.

2NN intrinsic-dimension instability at small W.
The 2-NN ID column has one infinite value (Paris_Corr_100)
and one near-zero value (Paris_Corr_10 at 0.103). These are numerical artefacts of running 2-NN intrinsic-dimension
estimation on a W-point set in R|V| — at small W the nearest-distance ratios are unstable. Diverse rows are more stable
(3.40 →11.16 →17.09 with W). The ID column is descriptive on diverse rows and artefact-prone on correlated rows; a
more stable estimator (e.g. TwoNN with a small-W correction or MLE-on-distance-ratios) would sharpen the correlated-
row values.
Scope of this benchmark.
The diagnostics are computed on the six-row bank (3 correlated and 3 diverse at W ∈
{3, 10, 100}) on a single gpt2 prior. The most material result is the reproduction of the simplex-∆sign-flip behavior
at W ≥10, which the main text flags as a finding that disagrees with the naive “correlated ⇒collapse” intuition. The
experiment surfaces the same finding under independent execution.
H.5
Autoregressive overlap trace and POS-class diagnostic on gpt2
The mixed coincidence partition function recurs at every step of a real autoregressive trace. Fixing W = 10 diverse
country-history prompts, sampling one shared continuation under gpt2, and broadcasting each generated token to every
prompt’s context, we compute at each step the W next-token log-priors and track log Zt(1) — which is 0 when all W
priors place their mass on the same token and large-negative when they disagree. The statistic tags each position by how
strongly the priors agree on the next token; we ask which positions carry that agreement by stratifying on the part-of-speech
of the surface token, tagged with spaCy en_core_web_sm 3.7.1 over a T = 40 generation.
63


## Page 64


Figure 10: Per-POS mean log Zt(1) on the realized W = 10 Paris_Div_10 continuation (T = 40), sorted by con-
sensus (0 = full 10-way agreement). The five function categories (PART, ADP, PUNCT, CCONJ, PRON) occupy the high-
consensus top; the content categories the low-consensus bottom. spaCy’s AUX tag here picks up modal/auxiliary verbs
whose continuation is content-loaded (“has been”, “have become”), so it sits with the content words (see text). Per-POS
counts (n, annotated) on this 40-token generation are small, so the means are point estimates without per-class CIs at this
realization.
Per-POS mean log Zt.
Figure 10 gives the per-POS-tag mean log Zt(1), sorted from most to least cross-prior consen-
sus.
Consensustrackstoken-levelpredictability, notsyntacticcategory.
Thestatisticseparatescleanlyalongthecontent–
function axis: the five function categories — particles, prepositions, punctuation, conjunctions, and pronouns, where the
next token is highly constrained — carry the highest cross-prior consensus, and the content categories the lowest. The
separation is about predictability of the token, not the syntactic class of the position: high log Zt requires that the W con-
text windows agree on the next token, which they do for function tokens like “of” or “the” and do not for content tokens.
The one apparent outlier sharpens this reading rather than complicating it: spaCy’s AUX category here contains exactly
the modal and auxiliary verbs (“has been”, “have become”) whose next token needs substantial context to predict, and it
sits at the bottom with the content words. A purely syntactic closed/open partition would mis-sort AUX to the top; the
content–function (predictability) grouping is the right one, and AUX is its confirming case.
Scope.
This is a single realization (W = 10, T = 40), so the per-POS means are point estimates; the closed-vs-open
Welch test quoted in the main text was computed on a separate W = 3 benchmark and is not reproduced here. The
direction of effect — function positions carry higher cross-prior consensus than content positions, with AUX confirming
that the diagnostic reads token predictability rather than syntactic class — is robust across the trace; a significance claim
at this single realization is not. The three-regime (Technical / Knowledge / Creative) variant is in Section H.7.
H.6
Synthetic Zipf phase-transition heatmap (Theorem 3.1)
This synthetic three-prior Zipf benchmark is the source of panel (a) of the body phase-transition figure (Figure 1); its
closed-form prior structure isolates parts (ii)–(iv) of Theorem 3.1 from any large-language-model implementation ef-
fect. The empirical multi-way coincidence probability over the (n, m) plane should transition across the threshold curve
m⋆(n) = Ψ(α) log n with Ψ(α) = |α|/(−log Z(α)); the gpt2-prior counterpart panels and registered evaluation are
at Section H.2.
64


## Page 65


Targets and grid.
Three priors over |V| = 10000 tokens are Zipfian rank-1.2 base distributions with i.i.d. N(0, 0.1)
logit noise per prior (seed 42), a moderately correlated benchmark. At multiplicities α = (2, 2, 2) (the body figure’s value
across all three benchmarks, so the per-benchmark Ψ are comparable), the realized log Z(α) ≈−9.62 gives Ψ(α) ≈
0.62. The (n, m) grid spans a 16-point geometric n ∈[50, 50000] and m ∈{1, . . . , 13}; each cell within a ±4-round
band of m⋆(n) runs T = 40 Monte-Carlo trials (off-band cells nearest-neighbor-filled at render so the heatmap reads
continuous), each trial sampling an n×m token matrix per population and testing α-coincidences by intersection across
the three populations.
Reading the heatmap.
Reading the color boundary of that panel along the dashed theoretical curve, the empirical
coincidence probability transitions from ≤0.1 to ≥0.9 within a band of width ≈1–2 rounds at every n-decade. The
transition is monotone in m at every n (no inverted cells where higher m has lower hit rate than its low-m neighbor),
and the location of the boundary is consistent with the predicted Ψ(α) log n scaling across the full three-decade n-range
[50, 50000].
Regime.
This benchmark is the correlated regime (logit-noise scale 0.1 produces three near-identical priors); the diverse-
prior counterpart is the gpt2-prior realization in panel (c) of Figure 1 and Section H.2.
H.7
Three-regime autoregressive coincidence trace on gpt2
Extending the autoregressive overlap trace of §6.1 to a Technical (constrained), Knowledge (semi-constrained), and Cre-
ative (unconstrained) continuation, each driven by W = 3 instruction-prefix variants over T = 40 tokens, the within-
trace variance of log Zt(1) orders as Technical (19.5) ≫Knowledge (5.6) ≈Creative (5.1) — the predicted direction,
since a more constrained continuation swings more widely between forced and soft positions. Knowledge and Creative
separate by their log Zt range (Creative never exceeds −6.8, uniformly diffuse; Knowledge spikes to −0.42 at consensus
function words), so the (Var, max) profile separates all three regimes. A single sampled continuation per regime is re-
alized, so the ordering matches the prediction qualitatively but is not significance-tested at n = 1 path per regime; the
canonical multi-prior trace is the POS-stratified realization of §H.5. The per-token trace figure is in the companion code
release.
H.8
Consensus as a single-feature open- vs. closed-class POS sensor
This experiment settles whether the stepwise multi-way coincidence diagnostic of §G.5 acts as a single-feature open- vs.
closed-class part-of-speech sensor. We run the autoregressive diagnostic across a model-scale axis — gpt2 (124M) and
EleutherAI/pythia at 70M, 160M, and 410M parameters — crossed with W ∈{3, 8} instruction prefixes, pool-
ing positions over five disjoint base prompts and three sampled continuations each (n from 242 to 506 positions per
configuration). At every position we tag the generated token’s part of speech and partition into the closed-class set
(DET, ADP, PUNCT, CCONJ, SCONJ, AUX, PART, PRON) and the open-class set (NOUN, VERB, ADJ, ADV, PROPN, INTJ, NUM). The
single-feature sensor quality is the ROC-AUC of a candidate consensus feature for the closed-vs-open classification:
chance is 0.50, and we read AUC ≥0.70 as a usable single-feature sensor.
Finding.
The full multiplicative consensus log Zt(1) resolves the open/closed split as a single-feature sensor in every
configuration: ROC-AUC ranges from 0.703 (410M) through 0.726 (70M) and 0.730 (gpt2) to 0.809 (160M), with Welch
p < 10−8 on the closed-vs-open mean comparison throughout and closed-class positions consistently higher in the con-
sensus — the direction predicted by the syntactic-constraint hypothesis. The coincidence divergence Φt, by contrast,
separates the classes only weakly (AUC 0.61–0.71), which is why a reading that keys on Φt alone finds the stratification
marginal. The two values of W are interchangeable (AUC differs by ≤0.005), and the AUC does not increase monoton-
ically with model scale: the sensor is already present at 70M parameters and at W = 3 and does not require scaling up
either axis. The diagnostic is therefore a single-feature POS sensor through the full-consensus feature, robustly across the
model scales tested.

H.10
De-Poissonization small-n correction
In the small-λ regime (λ ≤2) where the bare Poisson asymptote L1 = 1 −e−λ is loose, the exact finite-n residual of
the de-Poissonization lemma (Lemma F.3) improves the fit to the Monte-Carlo ground truth: on W = 3 Zipf priors at
α ∈{(2, 2, 2), (3, 2, 1)}, the exact-independent-cell form L3 is closer to Monte Carlo than L1 in 11 of 14 pre-registered
λ-cells, with the gain clearest at λ ∈[0.5, 1.5]. The Wilson 95% CI covers L3 in only 6/14 cells: at T = 103 trials the
estimator spread (≈0.015 near p ∼0.5) is comparable to the |L1 −L3| gap, so the CI resolves the two predictions only
where the gap is appreciable. Per-cell residuals and the figure are in the companion code release.
H.11
Atoms theorem: NNLS recovery of Renyi mixture from f-divergences
The atoms-of-typicality theorem (Theorem C.7) states that an f-divergence additive on independent products and obey-
ing the data-processing inequality is a positive mixture of Rényi divergences. A non-negative least-squares fit Df ≈
P
k wkDαk over a 25-point α-grid and N = 50 random pairs on |X| = 64 recovers the predicted Rényi-1 support for
KL (α⋆= 1.00, reconstruction residual 0, additivity residual 4.4×10−16) and fails cleanly on three non-additive controls
— χ2 and squared Hellinger (which are transformations of Rényi divergences, χ2 = eD2 −1, H2 = 1 −e−D1/2/2) and
total variation — whose additivity residuals range from 0.09 to 240. The test thus demarcates the theorem’s scope to
genuinely additive divergences. The recovery figure is in the companion code release.

each prior the renormalized expression sum with a 10−6 pseudocount) at α = 1, a single logsumexp call returns

H.13
Wider 60-neighborhood gpt2 benchmark: regime ordering at W = 3
We address the open follow-up at line 1204 of Section 5.2: the original Paris_Corr_3 vs. Paris_Div_3 benchmark was
3-prompt and 3-prompt; here we run the wider 30 + 30 neighborhood sweep on gpt2 (|V| = 50,257) and report the
typical regime ordering with bootstrap-BCa CIs over 30 neighborhoods per regime.
Pre-registered prompt bank.
30 correlated triplets, each sharing a Wikipedia-style fact (e.g. “Paris is the capital of” /
“The capital of France is” / “France’s capital is”), and 30 diverse triplets, each drawn from three unrelated topics (cooking
/ physics / finance). The bank is locked at the top of the eval script and is identical to the bank used in any later re-run.
Procedure.
For each neighborhood, evaluate gpt2’s next-token log-probability vector at the final position for each of
the three prompts, then evaluate the pooling-gap J(¯p) −Φ at the simplex centroid λ = (1/3, 1/3, 1/3), the energy
E(λ) = P
i λiH(p⋆
λ, πi), the top-K certified head fraction f(K) at K ∈{10, 50, 100}, log S2(p⋆
λ), and the multi-Rényi
Ψ(α) = log Z(α) at α = (2, 2, 2). Across the 30 per-regime neighborhoods we report the mean with a BCa 95% CI
(nresamples = 104) and the single-feature regime-classifier AUC (Mann–Whitney) with a percentile-bootstrap 95% CI.
Ψ-ordering resolution.
On the 3-prompt paper benchmark the realized Ψ-correlated vs. Ψ-diverse ordering reversed
relative to the original draft prediction; on the 30+30 wider bank we find the same direction (correlated > diverse, AUC
0.819withlowerbound 0.706 ≥0.70), withbootstrapCIseparationonthedifferenceofmedians. Theempiricalevidence
supports correlated-higher as the typical Ψ(2, 2, 2) ordering when the prior overlap is realized by correlated paraphrases.
Headline.
The pooling gap J(¯p)−Φ is the cleanest single-feature regime separator on the 30+30 wider bank at W = 3
on gpt2. The energy E(λ) separates regimes, but on this bank it is statistically weaker than the gap. The top-K head
fractions and log S2 do not separate regimes at the 95% level. The wider benchmark thus reduces the “single-feature
unsupervised diagnostic” claim of Section 5.2 to: the pooling-gap diagnostic separates correlated from diverse on the
wider bank with 95% CI on the difference of means strictly positive, with energy as a secondary separator that is regime-
typical but variance-dominated on the wider bank.

H.14
Continuum-α Riemann-sum convergence on gpt2 priors
Thecontinuum-indexedidentity(TheoremA.1)instantiatesonareal-LMpriorfamily: aparametricpathπθ = softmax(θ ℓ(0)+
(1 −θ) ℓ(1)) between two gpt2 final-position logit vectors, integrated against a Beta(2, 2) measure. Sweeping the Rie-

K) −ΦK| is exactly zero (machine precision)
at every K ≥16 on both a correlated and a diverse anchor pair, and the Riemann sum | log ZK −log Z1024| converges at
fitted rate r = 2.05 — the mid-point-rule O(1/K2) for a smooth integrand, above the r ≥1 target. The log Z1024 levels
separate the two pairs (−0.104 correlated vs. −0.777 diverse, a 0.67-nat gap recovering the regime ordering of §H.13 at
the continuum limit), but the convergence rate does not: gpt2’s final-position logits are dominated by a shared unembed-
ding bias (anchor cosines ≥0.9996 on both pairs), so the integrand has near-identical smoothness in both regimes and
the rate-separation predicted from anchor-cosine geometry is degenerate at this scale. The convergence figure and full
table are in the companion code release.
H.15
Guesswork log-moment vs. Rényi exponent: the ε = 0 specialization
We test the operational guesswork interpretation of Lemma 3.3 and (17): for ε = 0, the λ-th moment exponent of the
geometric hitting time G0(xn) ∼Geom(p0(xn)) equals λHα(P) with α = 1/(1 + λ).
Sources.
Three small-alphabet empirical distributions: (A) English-letter unigram (|X| = 26) from the Brown corpus;
(B) English-letter bigram (|X| = 676) from the same corpus; (C) Amino-acid frequencies (|X| = 20) from the overall
UniProt KB composition. All three have closed-form Rényi entropies at every α.
Procedure.
For each sourceand each λ ∈{0.5, 1, 2, 5}, at block-lengths n ∈{4, 6, 8, 10, 12}, wesample Nseq = 5000

MC standard error.
Cross-dataset reading.
The slope-versus-λ relation has the same monotone-superlinear shape across all three datasets,
with amplitude differences tracking the per-source Hα at each α = 1/(1 + λ): the empirical slopes of Table 6 track the
predicted λHα(P) within 5–8% on the two small-alphabet sources (Brown unigram, SwissProt amino acid) at every λ,
the bigram departure at intermediate λ being the pre-asymptotic finite-n deviation flagged in the failure-modes section.

H.16
Finite-n M-ary Rényi-Chernoff bound on real small-scale data
We test the finite-n M-ary pairwise Rényi-Chernoff upper bound (Lemma E.1, equation (47)) and the Arimoto–Rényi
conditional-entropy / Sason–Verdú admissible-band coupling (equation (45)) on three real small-alphabet multi-class
datasets, complementing the synthetic identity check of Section I and the mixed-norm identity verification of Section I.
The question is whether the bound stays above the empirical Bayes risk on real, not-exactly-categorical class-conditional
distributions, and how loose it is at the small block lengths n where the finite-n refinement matters most.
Setup.
Three pre-registered datasets, locked before any computation: (i) Latin-script letter unigram by language (Y =
Latin a–z, K = 26; M = 5 languages: English, German, Spanish, Italian, Dutch), estimated from the NLTK udhr
corpus (tiled to 50,000 letters per language); (ii) SCOP-class amino-acid composition (Y = the 20 standard amino acids,
K = 20; M = 4 structural classes: all-α, all-β, α/β, α + β), with 50,000 residues per class synthesized from published
class-conditional compositions; (iii) MNIST 8×8 coarse-binned pixel marginal (Y = binarized pixel indices on an 8 ×
8 downsampling of the original 28 × 28 image, K = 64; M = 10 digits), with 50,000 ON-pixel symbols per digit.
Block lengths n ∈{1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32}; Ntest = 20,000 blocks per class per cell; empirical-risk MAP rule applied to the
Jeffreys-smoothed bPi estimated from each per-class corpus. Class-conditional re-estimation noise propagated through
B = 200 BCa bootstrap resamples of the per-class corpus. Arimoto–Rényi HA
α (W|Y n) evaluated by stratified Monte
Carlo (nMC = 4,000 samples) on the product channel for α ∈{0.5, 2.0}, and the Sason–Verdú admissibility check
bεn ≥εlower(HA
α ; M) applied with the symmetric-channel inverse of the piecewise-tight relation.
Pre-registered failure modes.
The four failure modes anchored at proposal time are addressed as follows.
• Bound violated after the estimation-noise bootstrap. Does not occur on any of the 18 cells. The bootstrap-CI lower
edge of Bfull
n
exceeds the bootstrap-CI upper edge of bεn on every (dataset, n) cell, both with empirical and uniform
priors. The pairwise Rényi-Chernoff bound is valid on real not-exactly-categorical class-conditionals at the scales
tested.

• Tightness ratio explodes on MNIST coarse. The ratio is bounded by 5 at small n (M=10 ceiling), and decreases from
5.01 at n = 1 to 3.61 at n = 32 – the hardest pair takes over as n grows, exactly as the pairwise decomposition
predicts. The minimum pairwise Chernoff information Cmin = 0.0131 is small but non-trivial on this dataset,
giving a slow exponential decay of the bound; the ratio is informative, not vacuous.
• Estimation noise dominates. The bootstrap CI half-widths on Bfull
n
are between 0.001 and 0.02 – two orders of
magnitude narrower than the bound-vs-certificate gap on all three datasets. The observed looseness is a property
of the pairwise Rényi-Chernoff bound, not an artefact of mis-estimated class-conditionals; the conclusion of the
test (bound is valid and within a small-polynomial factor) is not estimation-noise-limited on the three datasets at the
per-class sample size 50,000. A modeling caveat: the letter-language unigram model treats letters as i.i.d. within a
block; real text has bigram and longer-range dependencies, so the empirical risk we measure is the misclassification
rate of a unigram-MAP classifier on bigram-correlated text, not the true Bayes risk of the language-ID task.
• Arimoto–Sibson band violated. Does not occur. For both α = 0.5 and α = 2.0, on all 18 cells, bεn+Wilson1/2 is at or
above the Sason–Verdú admissible lower envelope εlower(HA
α ; M) derived from inverting the symmetric-channel
piecewise-tight relation. The Arimoto–Rényi mixed-norm identity (verified to machine precision in Section I) ex-
tends to the real-data multi-class estimated-distribution regime with no observed band violation at the tested scales.
H.17
The multi-prior coincidence localizes regulatory elements at base-pair resolution
The multi-way coincidence calculus of Section 2 reads cell-type activity off the agreement between priors. Its most direct
genomic instantiation treats W = 3 independent sequence-to-function models — AlphaGenome, Enformer, and Borzoi
— as three independent observers of the same regulatory landscape. Their coincidence weight
Zc(ℓ) =
X
b
3
Y
m=1
πc
m(b)αm
is, for integer α = (1, 1, 1), exactly the probability that one genomic bin drawn from each model’s predicted track for
cell type c coincides: the agreement between independent predictors, in the natural direction. Monte-Carlo collision
counting (draw one bin per model, tally three-way coincidences) recovers the analytic weight Zc(ℓ) across all 132 locus-
by-cell-type cells, with log-scale correlation 0.73 — the partition function is a literal collision rate, not a heuristic score,
the genomic-scale instance of the coincidence Monte-Carlo of Section 5.3.
The coincidence track localizes the active element at base-pair scale.
The per-bin integrand Q
m πc
m(b) is itself a
genome track. We read it off the three models over the central 16 kb (128 bins of 128 bp) of three classic cell-type-specific
regulatory loci — the HBB locus-control region (erythroid; K562), the SFTPC promoter–enhancer (lung; A549), and the
ALB enhancer (liver; HepG2) — at the matched cell type and at a mismatched one (Figure 13). At the matched cell type
the three models concur on a single sharp peak that marks the active element: the effective support of the coincidence
track (the number of 128 bp bins holding half its mass) is a median of 7 bins across the three loci, as tight as 4 bins (512 bp)
at SFTPC. At a mismatched cell type the same track is diffuse (median 22 bins). The agreement of three independent
predictors collapses to a cell-type-specific, base-pair-localized element call; reading the same track for a mismatched cell
type returns a flat, uninformative landscape.
The localization is a multi-prior effect.
No single predictor resolves the element. A single model’s predicted track
over the same window is broad — effective support a median of 32 bins (range 24–41) — because each predictor sees
the whole open regulatory neighborhood, not the element within it. It is the coincidence of three independent priors, the
bins where all three concentrate together, that sharpens the diffuse single-predictor signal roughly 4–5× into a base-pair
element call (Figure 14). This is the multi-prior partition function doing exactly what the calculus predicts: a product over
independent factors is large only where every factor is, so coincidence is intrinsically a finer localizer than any one prior
— the genomic analogue of the closed-class consensus spikes of Section 6, now resolving a regulatory element rather than
a function word.
Scope.
Three loci carry full per-bin traces across all three models (the classic erythroid, lung, and liver elements); twenty-
two carry the summary collision and within-locus horizon statistics. The reported signal is the per-locus localization and
the matched-vs-mismatched contrast, not the absolute coincidence level, which is dominated by the locus rather than the

The same divergence, ranked across the panel, identifies the active cell type
The base-pair localization of Section H.17 suggests a cell-type classifier directly: if the multi-prior coincidence is sharp at
the matched cell type and diffuse at others, then the coincidence divergence Cc

Result: a 2–3× classifier.
Across two substrates (AlphaGenome, Enformer) crossed with two 90-locus candidate pan-
els — one literature-curated from cell-type-specific marker loci, one drawn at random from the ENCODE SCREEN
cCRE v3 distal-enhancer-like registry — the per-locus top-1 accuracy is 0.360, 0.489, 0.387, and 0.300, for a macro
mean of 0.384, which is 2.30× the six-class random baseline of 0.167; the strongest configuration (AlphaGenome ran-
dom cCRE, first-track-dominant weighting) reaches 0.556, a 3.33× lift. The top-1/top-2/top-3 accuracies separate the
substrates and panels: every top-1 value clears the 1/6 chance line, with AlphaGenome random cCRE strongest (top-1
0.49, top-3 0.79). A third model, Borzoi, reproduces the pattern, and on every substrate both the in-distribution curated
panel and the unbiased random panel clear chance — so the signal is neither a marker-curation artefact nor specific to
one predictive model.
Structure and limits.
The accuracy is non-uniform: HepG2, MCF-7, and K562 are reliably identified (0.67–0.73 on
the strongest run, two-to-three standard errors above chance at 15 loci per cell type), while H1 (embryonic stem cell) and
GM12878 are weakest, with recurring confusions of H1 with K562 and of GM12878 with HepG2. The binary framing of
the same diagnostic — whether c’s statistic is systematically larger at c-specific loci than elsewhere — sits near chance in
aggregate cross-loci ROC AUC, because that marginal comparison is dominated by the genome-wide baseline of c’s tracks;
the per-locus cross-cell-type ranking is the discriminating quantity, and the two should not be conflated. The signal is
substantial but not overwhelming, and a single model’s six-cell-type panel is the resolution ceiling here, not the calculus:
the cross-model coincidence of Section H.17, which localizes the element to base-pair scale, is the sharper instrument.

H.19
Boundary-detection on regulatory landscapes via cell-type accessibility priors
This supplement sharpens Section 6.2 by replacing the (6-mer × cCRE-class) joint prior of Table 2 with a cell-type ac-
cessibility categorical prior over an eight-cell ENCODE DNase panel (GM12878, K562, HepG2, HeLa-S3, IMR-90, H1,
MCF-7, A549; ENCODE “read-depth normalized signal” bigWigs at GRCh38), on three loci chosen for clean proximal-
promoter / cell-type-specific-enhancer juxtaposition: MYC (chr8:127.6–128.4 Mb; broadly active proximal promoter at
chr8:127,736,231withthecell-type-specificBENCandPVT1-regiondistal-enhancerclustersdownstream), ALB(chr4:73.2–
73.6Mb; canonicalliver-specificgenewithHepG2-dominantaccessibilityflankedbyAFM/AFP/AHSG),andGAPDH(chr12:6.4–
6.65 Mb; canonical housekeeping promoter with flanking cell-type-specific dELS calls). For each 2,048 bp window at 256
bp stride, we form π(ct | window) ∝max(0, sct −bct) + ε where sct is the mean window DNase signal and bct is
the panel-mean over 104 random size-matched hg38 windows. The W = 3 adjacent-window neighborhood and the
log-domain partition function machinery of Theorem 2.1 are reused unchanged.
Headline.
The pooling-gap diagnostic |J(¯p) −Φ|, the P01-native quantity from Section 6.2, is the boundary detector
that “screams” on this prior: it reaches ROC-AUC 0.938 (95% BCa CI [0.901, 0.959]) on ALB, 0.894 ([0.661, 0.963]) on
MYC, and 0.863 ([0.710, 0.972]) on GAPDH for the detection of constitutive-to-cell-type-specific transitions (boundary
truth: pairs of windows whose entropy on the cell-type categorical lies in the top 5% and the bottom 20% of the locus,
encountered within ±2 windows). The mean across the three loci is 0.899 and the maximum is 0.938; both meet the
0.897 joint-cCRE benchmark of Table 2 and the 0.938 ALB value strictly exceeds it. Bootstrap-BCa intervals come from
104 resamples on the per-window diagnostic-vs-label vector. Compute is 24 parallel CPU cells (one per (locus, cell type)
pair), 82.2 s end-to-end wall-clock for the fanout.
Reading the divergence channels.
A complementary observation is that DKL
 ¯p ∥¯ppanel−uniform

is anti-correlated
with the same boundary truth (mean AUC 0.348, max 0.430) – because at a boundary the W = 3 pooled ¯p is a mixture of
one constitutive (near-uniform) window with one or more cell-type-specific (low-entropy) windows, giving an interme-
diate divergence to the panel-uniform reference. The two channels therefore agree with the P01 intuition that Φt at the
segmentation scale measures within-neighborhood disagreement (which spikes at boundaries), whereas single-window di-
vergence to a constant reference measures regional cell-type purity (which has its extrema in the interiors of the regions on
either side). ¯ppanel−uniform is therefore the wrong reference for boundary detection on this prior; the W-fold log-pooled
partition function is what the calculus of Theorem 2.1 prescribes, and what the data confirm.
Caveats.
The headline ≥0.95 criterion is missed at the mean level (0.899) but met at the peak (0.938 on ALB). The MYC
and GAPDH bootstrap intervals are wide because the entropy-derived boundary truth picks out only n+ = 12 and n+ = 4
positive-class windows respectively at those loci; the ALB confidence interval is narrow at [0.901, 0.959] on n+ = 19. The
boundary truth is derived from the cell-type-prior alphabet itself (entropy quantiles on the W = 3 pooled distribution),
and is therefore a diagnostic test that the calculus resolves prior-visible structure rather than a held-out prediction task.

Closed-form evaluation.
We evaluate the bonus term Cα in closed form from (61) on cached LM logits, rather than by
LoRA fine-tuning Pythia-1.4B on a SuperGLUE-style multi-task benchmark and measuring the empirical generalization
gap against the multi-prior PAC-Bayes bound. The closed-form route evaluates the bonus exactly; it does not measure the
empirical generalization gap. Three sources of fidelity loss are disclosed: (i) Pythia-160M was used in place of the spec’s
Pythia-1.4B; smaller models have higher entropy next-token distributions, which inflates pairwise overlap and thus Cα –

contribution Cα and the bound-penalty-decomposition identity (61) are evaluated.
Tasks.
Three task families with W = 3 paraphrased prompts each at α = (1/3, 1/3, 1/3): factual capitals (“The capital

30 entities each, 90 within-task triples plus 30 across-task triples (one paraphrase pulled from each of the three different
tasks).
Magnitude of the bonus.
On Pythia-160M with the synthetic posterior, the coincidence-bonus Cα contributes 1 –
9% of the single-prior PAC-Bayes penalty across the three within-task regimes, and ≈8.8% in the across-task regime.
The factual_capitals task has the smallest bonus (median 0.096 nats), the two other within-task regimes intermediate
(factual_chemistry 0.543, factual_arithmetic 0.516 nats), and the across-task triples the largest (median 0.778 nats). The
bonus is non-zero on all 120 triples, never approaches minw D(ρ∥πw) (i.e., the multi-prior bound is not catastrophically
tightened), and is bounded above by P

Regime-separationhypothesisiscontradictedatthisscale.
Thepre-registeredintuition—correlatedpriors(within-
task paraphrases of the same fact) should produce a LARGER coincidence bonus than diverse priors (across-task triples
mixing unrelated topics) — is contradicted by the closed-form measurements. Bootstrap CI on the median bonus-ratio
difference within-task minus across-task is −0.030 [−0.054, −0.023]; the CI excludes zero on the WRONG side. The
mechanism is that within-task paraphrases concentrate next-token mass on a small argmax-region (the gold token), and
concentrated priors share less under the geometric tilt than priors whose mass is spread more uniformly across the vocab-
ulary. The across-task triples have higher entropy in each πw and so the geometric mixture’s normalizer Z(α) extracts
more overlap.
This is a substantive empirical signal: the four-role-fusion claim in P01 (the geometric mixture p⋆
α is simultane-
ously a max-entropy optimum, a KL-barycenter minimum, a Boltzmann coincidence weight, and a PAC-Bayes-penalty-
tightening prior) holds as an identity at every triple (the residual is at machine precision); but the regime in which the
bonus is OPERATIONALLY largest is the high-entropy, low-correlation regime, not the intuitively correlated one.
Reading.
ThePAC-Bayescoincidence-bonusistheanalyticalcontentofthemulti-priorboundfromP01PropositionC.3;
this experiment verifies the proposition’s closed-form decomposition to machine precision and measures the bonus mag-
nitude on a real LM benchmark for the first time. The bonus is non-vacuous (always positive, 0.1 – 1.0 nats across the
four regimes) and tightens the bound by 1 – 9% of the single-prior PAC-Bayes penalty at W = 3 with simplex-centroid
weights. The PAC-Bayes story’s strongest reading — that the bound is meaningfully tightened by the coincidence bonus
on real LM neighborhoods — is supported. The reading that the bonus is largest on intuitively correlated priors is not
supported and reverses on Pythia-160M. The Pythia-1.4B scale-up and the empirical-gap measurement remain open; the
closed-form identity holds at every scale by the proposition, so what scale-up contributes is a tighter quantitative estimate
of the bonus magnitude under more concentrated priors.
H.21
hg38 / ENCODE pharmacogene re-benchmark: stage-1 composition baseline
We benchmark the headline genomic diagnostic of Section 6.2 on 1.87 Mb of exact hg38 sequence: 3,636 windows of
2,048 bp at stride 512 bp across four pharmacogene loci (CYP2D6, CYP3A4, ABCB1, CYP2B6), via a two-stage strategy.
Two-stage strategy.
The full benchmark requires ∼4 GB of reference data (hg38 FASTA, ENCODE/SCREEN Registry
V4 cCRE annotation, HepG2 DNase-seq bigWig) plus optional DNA language-model (Caduceus / HyenaDNA) inference.
We adopt a two-stage strategy. Stage-1 (this section) runs a 6-mer composition baseline on synthetic windowed-genome
data and reports whether stage-2 (DNA-LM inference on real hg38) is necessary; the synthetic-data baseline avoids the
∼4 GB data-staging the full run would otherwise require.
74


## Page 75


Figure 15: Coincidence-bonus Cα/ minw D(ρ∥πw) across the four triple regimes. The within-task triples show smaller
bonus magnitudes than the across-task triples; the regime-separation hypothesis is contradicted at the 95% level. The
closed-form identity (61) is verified to machine precision on every triple.
Stage-1 method.
Simulate N = 4 cCRE-like sequence classes (background, PLS, pELS, dELS surrogates) via Dirichlet-
perturbed 6-mer multinomials, sample 3,636 windows total in run-length stretches, build window-triples (l, c, r) and
label each by whether the center window’s class differs from its neighbors (stable / boundary). On each triple compute
the pooling gap J(¯p) −Φ at W = 3 on (a) the 4,096-dimensional 6-mer prior alphabet (sequence-only) and (b) the
joint alphabet formed by appending a 5-class k-means-derived putative cCRE tag (|Vjoint| = 20,480, matching the spec).
Report ROC AUC of the pooling gap as boundary-detection score with bootstrap-BCa 95% CI (nresamples = 2,000).
Stage-1 result.
On the synthetic windowed-genome the joint-alphabet pooling gap reaches a boundary-detection ROC
AUC of 0.968 (bootstrap-BCa 95% CI [0.959, 0.976]), above the ≥0.85 target, while the sequence-only AUC saturates
at 1.000 — a sign that the synthetic regime is too clean to reproduce the paper’s sequence-only difficulty (Figure 16).
Stage-2 decision: UNCLEAR.
The stage-1 result on the synthetic regime cannot definitively settle whether stage-2
(DNA-LM inference at the four pharmacogene loci) is necessary. The synthetic data is too clean: the sequence-only AUC
saturates at 1.000, whereas the paper’s claim depends on real hg38 windows being non-trivially harder than the joint
alphabet. Two readings are admissible:
(a) The k-mer baseline is the right floor: on real hg38 the sequence-only AUC drops from 1.000 to the paper-reported
≈0.567 because real 6-mer composition is noisier within class than the Dirichlet-perturbed synthetic data; the
joint alphabet gain depends on whether the cCRE-class tag carries discriminative information beyond 6-mer com-
position. Whether the joint-alphabet AUC reaches the paper-claimed 0.897 on real hg38 is the question stage-2 is
registered to answer.
(b) If a DNA-LM (Caduceus / HyenaDNA) inference on real hg38 returns a sequence-only AUC similar to the k-mer
baseline’s real-data floor, then the language model offers no improvement over the simpler k-mer feature; if the
DNA-LM substantially outperforms the k-mer baseline, the language-model representation is doing real work.
The auditable next step is the Modal-tier stage-2 run on real hg38 data, with the k-mer baseline retained as the
sequence-only floor against which the DNA-LM and the joint alphabet are benchmarked. The synthetic stage-1 verifies
that the analysis pipeline (window-triple enumeration, pooling-gap statistic on a 4096-dim prior, joint-alphabet construc-
tion via k-means surrogate, bootstrap-BCa AUC CI) is wired end-to-end and producing operationally sensible numbers;
the real-data application is what stage-2 will measure.

The full original spec (per-locus stratification at CYP2D6 / CYP3A4 / ABCB1 / CYP2B6, cross-locus
Spearman matrix, the six metrics Φ, J(¯p)−Φ, K95, S2, C(1,1,1), pcoinc) is gated on a Modal-tier dispatch with hg38 FASTA

H.22
Held-out NLL: geometric vs. arithmetic pooling on gpt2 next-token tasks
We benchmark the ML-practitioner-grade claim of P01 that geometric pooling p⋆
λ ∝Q
i πλi
i
attains the KL-barycenter
optimum exactly, whereas the arithmetic mixture ¯p does not, and that the pooling-gap diagnostic J(¯p) −Φ predicts the
per-context NLL improvement of geometric over arithmetic pooling.
Tasks.
Five pre-registered next-token tasks — capitals, largest-planet, color-of-thing, chemical-symbol, arithmetic —
each constructed as templates with three paraphrase prompts at W = 3. For every context we measure the next-token
NLL of the gold-token first BPE id under (a) each single-prior πi, (b) the arithmetic mixture ¯p, (c) the geometric mixture p⋆
λ
at the simplex centroid, (d) the W = 2 contrastive specialization p ∝π1/π2. Per-task N ranges from 20 to 200, limited
by the entity pool for each template; across the five tasks 464 contexts contribute to the table below.
Cost-reduced implementation.
This experiment was implemented as the cost-reduced variant of the registered spec;
the cost-reduction technique is on-disk caching of gpt2 next-token log-prob distributions keyed by SHA1(prompt), so
the analysis layer is decoupled from the inference layer. Fidelity loss assessment: the cached logits are bit-exact reuses of
the same gpt2-small forward pass that a Modal-tier dispatch would produce; the only fidelity gap relative to the original
spec is the limited entity pool for two of the five tasks (largest_planet at n = 20, color_of_thing at n = 69).
The capitals reversal at full scale.
The capitals reversal already flagged at N = 20 in the truncated benchmark —
geometric is significantly worse than arithmetic — is confirmed at the full-scale benchmark: ∆NLL = +0.46 nats, CI
[+0.376, +0.544], n = 181. The mechanism, identified in the truncated benchmark, is reproduced and tightened: the
third paraphrase (“The capital city of ⟨country⟩is the city of”) is the longest and most natural-language, concentrates more
probability mass on the correct capital, and the arithmetic mean inherits its strength. The geometric mixture at equal
weights gives all three priors equal multiplicative weight in log-space and is dragged below the strongest single prior.
The arithmetic task exhibits the same heterogeneously-aligned-priors failure mode at the full benchmark (∆= +0.22,
CI [+0.166, +0.272], n = 97). The two reversal tasks share the structural feature that one of the three paraphrases is
noticeably more gold-aligned than the other two; on the three other tasks (color, chemistry, planet) the paraphrases are
comparably aligned, and either geometric ties arithmetic or strictly beats it.

Table 7: Per-task held-out next-token NLL of gpt2 under five pooling rules. “Best single” is the per-context minimum
NLL across the three single-prior baselines. The paired-bootstrap 95% CI on NLLgeom −NLLarith is reported in the
last column (nresamples = 5000). Geometric pooling strictly beats arithmetic (CI below zero) on 2/5 tasks (color-of-
thing, chemical-symbol); arithmetic strictly beats geometric on 2/5 (capitals, arithmetic); the difference is statistically
indistinguishable on 1/5 (largest-planet, at n = 20 underpowered). On none of the five tasks does geometric pooling
beat the per-context best single prior (the harder pre-registered bar). The pre-registered success bar (geometric beats
arithmetic on ≥4 tasks, beats best single on ≥3 tasks) is not met on this benchmark; the headline ML-practitioner claim
is partially supported on two of the five tasks and contradicted on two.
Contrastive decoding baseline.
The α = (1, −1) specialization p ∝π1/π2 is far worse than any other pooling rule
on all five tasks (NLL ≥7.9 on every task, ∼2× the geometric / arithmetic NLL). The chosen base/contrast pair is two
paraphrase priors of a single model, not the strong-vs-weak setting that contrastive decoding is designed for; the result is
consistent with the pre-registered failure mode for this baseline.
Pool-gap prediction.
The pool-gap quintile stratification (per task, each task split into five gap quintiles with the per-
quintile mean of NLLgeom −NLLarith as the response) gives a clearer signal at N = 200 than at N = 20: on the two
reversal tasks (capitals, arithmetic) the per-quintile mean of NLLgeom −NLLarith stays positive across quintiles, with the
upper quintiles widening the gap; on the two tasks where geometric beats arithmetic (color, chemistry) the per-quintile
mean is negative across quintiles and grows in magnitude with the gap. The reversal tasks therefore have a pool-gap that
correctly identifies them as cases where pooling does not reduce NLL; the gap-as-monotone-predictor reading does hold
at this granularity, but its sign depends on whether the heterogeneous-priors failure mode is active.
Reading.
The geometric-mixture optimality result is an identity-level statement at the simplex centroid for the KL-
barycenter Φ(λ); the held-out NLL of the next gold token is a different population quantity. The two coincide only when
the geometric mixture is a good model of the held-out distribution. The N = 200 benchmark confirms a sharp dichotomy:
on tasks with priors of comparable gold-alignment quality, geometric pooling has a strict NLL edge over arithmetic; on
tasks where one prior dominates the others, the arithmetic mean exploits the dominant prior and beats geometric pooling.
The headline ML-practitioner claim that “geometric beats arithmetic by an NLL-positive amount” is supported on 2/5
tasks and contradicted on 2/5. Geometric pooling never beats the per-context best single prior, which is the harder pre-
registered bar.
I
Numerical verification of the identity and its specializations
This appendix records the verification of the mixed coincidence identity and its algebraic specializations at machine preci-
sion. These are implementation-correctness checks: the object of interest is that each predicted equality holds at floating-
point precision, evidence that the log-domain pipeline computes both sides correctly, not evidence of a phenomenon.
The contribution is the identity itself (Theorem 2.1) and the calculus it organizes; the exhaustive per-cell residual records
summarized below are released in full with the companion code.
The core identity and its four roles.
Across a synthetic sweep over the cell parameters (W, |X|, α-regime, scaling),
the log-partition log Z(α) computed along two independent numerical paths — a logsumexp accumulation and an
extended-precision sorted sum — agrees to float64 machine precision, the worst residual over 128 cells being 3.55 ×
10−15 (≈16 units in the last place) and the mean at the single-ULP level. The worst cells are exactly the documented
catastrophic-cancelationregimes: high-multiplicityW = 8, |X| = 50,000integermultiplicities, andnegative-component
α from the contrastive regime. The four roles the identity fuses — Boltzmann coincidence weight, exponential-family
77


## Page 78


log-normalizer, constrained maximum-entropy value, and KL-barycenter optimum — agree to a worst pairwise residual
of 3.05 × 10−15 over 50 instances, with the geometric mixture the unique minimizer on every instance. The continuum-
indexed extension specializes exactly to the discrete identity at finite support, and its smooth-α Riemann sum converges
at the midpoint-rule rate O(1/K2) (fitted slope −2.0).
Axioms, decompositions, and the classical corollaries.
The multi-way coincidence divergence Cα = −log Z(α)
satisfies all six asserted axiomatic properties — nonnegativity, permutation invariance, joint convexity in the priors,
the data-processing inequality, tensorization, and the minimax / information-radius characterization — across 210 syn-
thetic instances, with the strict-equality axioms at the float64 floor and the inequality axioms carrying strictly positive
slack. The sharp Sanov / Gibbs-conditioning decomposition’s leading exponent upper-bounds the exactly-enumerated
empirical-distribution probability on every cell, with a bounded polynomial residual. The Donsker–Varadhan equality
and its sub-Gaussian transportation inequality hold at the Gibbs tilt to machine precision with the predicted positive
transportation slack. The exponential-family Jensen-gap form of Λij(α), the moment-matching Pythagorean equality
at the information projection, and the multi-prior PAC-Bayes coincidence-bonus decomposition each reproduce their
closed form at the single-ULP level (worst residual 2.44 × 10−15 over the 1080-cell PAC-Bayes sweep). The KL (α = 1)
Pythagorean identity holds at machine precision; the strict α-Pythagorean at α ̸= 1, which requires the α-projection
rather than the KL e-projection, is left to a future implementation.
Mixed-norm forms and scaling laws.
The Arimoto–Rényi conditional entropy and the Sibson α-mutual information
mixed-norm representations compute the same functionals as their standard posterior-norm and tilt definitions to the
single-ULP level across a 2160-cell sweep. Two scaling predictions hold at the synthetic scale: the multiplicative-cascade
separation depth eα(n, ε) tracks Ψ(α) log n to within 6%, and the Bahadur–Rao saddlepoint prefactor ratio converges
monotonically to one across low-, mid-, and high-tail thresholds (with float64 underflow only beyond n = 2000 at the
highest tail). The synthetic M-ary pairwise Rényi–Chernoff bound is valid on all 540 cells with a tightness ratio between
one and five, and the prior-weighted constant M−1
2
+ 1
2
P
i<j |πi −πj| strictly improves the crude (M −1) factor on
every skewed-prior cell; its real-data instantiation is in Section H.16.
The (1, −1) specialization is contrastive decoding.
The (1, −1) specialization of the typical distribution is numer-
ically identical to contrastive decoding [56]: across 20 prompts over the full gpt2 vocabulary the per-token log-score
residual sits at float64 machine epsilon and the top-K ranker agreement is unity at every plausibility threshold and
cutoff. The two are the same scoring rule, not an approximation of one another.
The identity at LLM and genomic scale.
On the realized Paris benchmark of Section 5.2 (reported with Figure 8)
the residual between the geometric-mixture candidate and the closed-form Φ(λ) holds at floating-point precision across
every row and every W ∈{3, 10, 100}, with the slight degradation expected as more log-priors enter the log-sum-exp
accumulation, and it continues to hold as the effective vocabulary is varied by truncating each prior to a score-top-K head
and renormalizing — matching the analytic guarantee that the identity is non-asymptotic and resolution-independent.
The same holds on the real hg38 benchmark of Section 6, on both the sequence-only and joint sequence–cCRE alphabets
and across all windowing regimes. A separately-implemented reference notebook reproduces the identity on an indepen-
dent small benchmark at the float32 precision of its torch pipeline. These checks confirm that the empirical findings
of Sections 5 and 6 do not rest on a silent normalization drift or log-sum-exp underflow.
